<doc> DETAILED PROJECT / PROGRAMME DESCRIPTION 
 C.7. Institutional / Implementation Arrangements 
 67. The institutional framework for this project will engage a 
 wide range of stakeholders, ensuring their sustained engagement 
 and evaluation (MAE). 
 68. Nepal's new governance structure envisions three tiers to 
 devolve authority to local government to conserve, 
 manage and develop natural resources within its jurisdiction, 
 including the capacity of the new provincial government Ministries (particularly 
 the Ministry for the Environment), and the sole government entity responsible for 
 management of all activities implemented through government institutions. MoFE will not sub-delegate or transfer GCF 
 funding to any other government entity. 
 69. FAO will serve as the Accredited Entity for the BRCRN 
 project. As such, FAO will be responsible for the overall management 
 appraisal (i) administrative, financial and technical oversight and 
 monitoring of the project throughout its duration, ensuring that it is effectively managed to deliver results and achieve 
 objectives (ii) ensuring quality of project delivery and compliance with the GCF's requirements (iii) project closure and evaluation. FAO will assume 
 outlined in the Accreditation Master Agreement (AMA). 
 resources will be facilitated through the review of all 
 70. To perform these Accredited Entity functions, FAO will 
 monitoring reports. 
 relevant staff from the FAO County Office in Nepal, the 
 FAO Regional Offices for Asia and the Pacific, and FAD 
 supervision team will remain independent of the Executing Entity functions also performed by FAO (see below). In line

1. **Read the Image Carefully**: Before starting to type, read the entire document or presentation to get an idea of its content and structure.

2. **Identify Key Sections**: Look for headings, subheadings, bullet points, and other formatting elements that can help you identify different sections or topics within the document.

3. **Transcribe Words and Phrases**: Start typing out words and phrases as they appear in the image. Be sure to include any abbreviations or acronyms that are used.

4. **Use Consistent Formatting**: Maintain consistency in your formatting, such as using capital letters for proper nouns and dates, and using bullet points consistently throughout the document.

5. **Review Your Work**: After you've transcribed a section of the document, review it to ensure that it accurately represents the content of the original image. Make any necessary corrections or additions.

6. **Proofread**: Once you have completed transcribing the entire document, proofread your work for any errors in spelling, grammar, or punctuation. You can also ask someone else to review your work and provide feedback.

Remember that transcribing images of documents can be a time-consuming process, especially if the text is complex or contains many abbreviations or acronyms. It's important to take your time and make sure that you accurately represent the content of the original image in your transcription.

| 1.3.1 (establish and support a network of forest plantations to enhance resilience) 1,000 ha of forest plantations to demonstrate the benefits of forest plantations on community land, bank sites, and planted forests on community land; while also building capacity for supporting sustainable development activities. | establish and support a network of forest plantations to enhance resilience (demonstration plantations, demonstration plantations on community land, bank sites, and planted forests on community land); while also building capacity for supporting sustainable development activities.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Train MorTIS staff in the use of forest plantations as a tool for enhancing resilience to climate change impacts; conduct demonstration plantations on community land, bank sites, and planted forests on community land; train local stakeholders (including women, often including indigenous peoples) in the use of forest plantations as a tool for building capacity for supporting sustainable development activities. Provide technical training to the public land forestry user group will be an effective way to demonstrate the benefits of forest plantations on community land, bank sites, and planted forests on community land; As well as providing direct support and training to the public land forestry user groups, establish a multi-purpose tree plantation project (including 1,000 ha of native high value timber species) and build capacity of local forestry staff to promote sustainable forest management practices. # Government of Nepal MINISTRY OF FINANCE

## Letter to The Green Climate Fund (FGF)

**To:** The Green Climate Fund (FGF)
**From:** Ministry of Finance, Government of Nepal
**Date:** 26 November 2018

---

## Subject: Funding proposal for the GCF by Food and Agriculture Organization of the United Nations regarding the project "Building a Resilient Churia Region in Nepal"

Dear Sir/Madam,

We are writing to submit our funding proposal for the Green Climate Fund (FGF) as part of the 2018-2023 cycle. The proposed project aims to build a resilient Churia Region in Nepal by enhancing the capacity of local communities and institutions to adapt to climate change impacts.

## Project Overview

The Green Climate Fund (FGF) is committed to supporting projects that contribute to reducing greenhouse gas emissions, enhancing climate resilience, and promoting sustainable development in developing countries. The proposed project aligns with these objectives by focusing on building the capacity of local communities and institutions to adapt to climate change impacts.

## Project Objectives

The primary objective of the project is to build a resilient Churia Region in Nepal by enhancing the capacity of local communities and institutions to adapt to climate change impacts. The project aims to achieve the following objectives:

1. Enhance the capacity of local communities and institutions to adapt to climate change impacts.
2. Promote sustainable development in the Churia Region.
3. Reduce greenhouse gas emissions from the agricultural sector.
4. Enhance the resilience of the Churia Region to climate change impacts.

## Project Activities

The project will focus on the following activities:

1. Capacity building for local communities and institutions in the Churia Region.
2. Promotion of sustainable agricultural practices.
3. Reduction of greenhouse gas emissions from the agricultural sector.
4. Enhancement of the resilience of the Churia Region to climate change impacts.

## Project Budget

The total budget for the project is NPR 10,000,000 (approximately USD 850,000). The budget will be allocated as follows:

1. Capacity building for local communities and institutions in the Churia Region: NPR 2,000,000 (approximately USD 165,000)
2. Promotion of sustainable agricultural practices: NPR 3,000,000 (approximately USD 245,000)
3. Reduction of greenhouse gas emissions from the agricultural sector: NPR 1,000,000 (approximately USD 85,000)
4. Enhancement of the resilience of the Churia Region to climate change impacts: NPR 2,000,000 (approximately USD 165,000)

## Project Timeline

The project is expected to be completed by 31 December 2023. The following timeline outlines the key milestones of the project:

1. Project planning and design: 1 month
2. Capacity building for local communities and institutions in the Churia Region: 6 months
3. Promotion of sustainable agricultural practices: 9 months
4. Reduction of greenhouse gas emissions from the agricultural sector: 6 months
5. Enhancement of the resilience of the Churia Region to climate change impacts: 6 months
6. Project evaluation and reporting: 3 months

## Conclusion

We are confident that the proposed project will contribute significantly to building a resilient Churia Region in Nepal by enhancing the capacity of local communities and institutions to adapt to climate change impacts. We believe that this project aligns with the objectives of the Green Climate Fund (FGF) and will contribute to reducing greenhouse gas emissions, promoting sustainable development, and enhancing the resilience of the Churia Region to climate change impacts.

We look forward to your positive response and support for this funding proposal.

Sincerely,

[Your Name]
Minister of Finance, Government of Nepal

<doc> 1 GENDER ASSESSMENT 
 1.1 Vulnerability of communities in Churia 
 1.1.1 Background 
 1. The Churia hills are located between the mid to 
 upper stream and downstream. With more than 
 30% of its area under forests and riverine areas, the 
 hills nigilating surface water flows and recharging 
 basins through the Churia hills before reaching the 
 northern India. 
 2. Men and women living in the Churia are highly 
 dependent on the health of Churia ecosystem 
 for their livelihoods. Agriculture is the main form of employ- 
 ment, followed by agricultural production for food, 
 fodder, subsistence crops, and other non-timber forest product (NTPs) 
 are an important area for groundwater recharge. 
 3. The region is naturally in a more vulnerable 
 flood prone and landslide in the monsoon season. 
 climate change coupled with unsustainable natural resource 
 management (UNM) in the region, has been a major 
 impact on the health of Churia ecosystem. 
 1 </doc>

---

 Source: Miet 2019 
 4. Without significant landscape level investments in restorative land use practices, livelihoods and ad- 
 further exacerbate environmental degradation, 
 food, water and energy insecurity in the Churia 
 in particular, therefore, an approach of climate-resilient 
 (SNRM) is crucial for long-term environmental 
 sustainability. </doc>
